  This is the second item.

  To prove the fourth item, we begin by noting that for any $x,y\in W$ with $|x - y|\leqslant\ell$ we have the identity
  \begin{equation}
    V(x)\cdot\nabla\Psi(y) - |\nabla\Psi(x)| = V(x)\cdot(\nabla\Psi(y) - \nabla\Psi(x)) + \Big(V(x) - \frac{\nabla\Psi(x)}{|\nabla\Psi(x)|}\Big)\cdot\nabla\Psi(x)
  \end{equation}
  and hence, from~\eqref{e_2} and~\eqref{e_3}, the estimate 
  \begin{equation}\label{e_4}
    |V(x)\cdot\nabla\Psi(y) - |\nabla\Psi(x)||\leqslant\frac54\omega(\ell) + 2\Big(\frac{\max_{\widetilde{W}}|\nabla\Psi|}{\min_{\widetilde{W}}|\nabla\Psi|}\Big)^2\omega(\ell)
  \end{equation}
  is valid. By taking $\ell>0$ smaller, if necessary, we can arrange from~\eqref{e_4} that
  \begin{equation}
    |V(x)\cdot\nabla\Psi(y) - |\nabla\Psi(x)||\leqslant\frac18\min_M|\nabla\Psi|
  \end{equation} 
  and so estimate~\eqref{looking_at} holds.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- lem-12: body_md -->
\label{lem on regularized distance to the manifold}
    Define the complete metric spaces
    \begin{equation}\label{complete_metric_spaces}
        \mathcal{X} = \{{g\in C^0_{\mathrm{b}}(W_\varepsilon\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon])\;:\;\lvg\rVert_{C^0_{\mathrm{b}}(W_\varepsilon\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon])}\leqslant\lambda}\}\text{ and }\mathcal{B} = \{{\phi\in C^1_{\mathrm{b}}(\overline{U})\;:\;\lVert\phi\rVert_{C^{0,1}(\overline{U})}\leqslant r}\}.
    \end{equation}
    There exists a Lipschitz continuous mapping $\mathbf{f}:\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{X}$ such that the following hold for all $\phi\in\mathcal{B}$ and all $(x,t)\in W_\varepsilon\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]$.
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item Upon defining
        \begin{equation}\label{occasionally_useful}
             G_\phi(x,t) = x + V(x)[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t),\text{ we have the inclusion }G_\phi(x,t)\in W_{2^{-37}\ell}.
        \end{equation}
        \item We have the equality
        \begin{equation}\label{projection_parametrization_identity}
            (\Psi + \phi)\circ(x + V(x)[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t)) = t.
        \end{equation}
        \item We have the estimate
        \begin{equation}\label{distance to the manifold bound}
            \frac{1}{2\beta}|\Psi(x) + \phi(x) - t|\leqslant|[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t)|\leqslant2\beta|\Psi(x) + \phi(x) - t|.
        \end{equation}
        \item If $(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})\in W_\varepsilon\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]$ satisfy $|x - \widetilde{x}|\leqslant\lambda/2$, we have the Lipschitz estimate
        \begin{equation}\label{initial_lip_est}
            |[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t)-[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})|\leqslant6\beta|x - \widetilde{x},t - \widetilde{t}|.
        \end{equation}
        \item We have that $\mathbf{f}(\phi)\in C^1_{\mathrm{b}}(W_\varepsilon\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon])$; moreover the mapping
        \begin{equation}
            \mathcal{B}\ni\widetilde{\phi}\mapsto \mathbf{f}(\widetilde{\phi}) \in C^1_{\mathrm{b}}(W_\varepsilon\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon])\text{ is continuous}.
        \end{equation}
    \end{enumerate}
<!-- end lem-12 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
We argue via the contraction mapping principle. Seeking a solution to the level set projection-parametrization identity~\eqref{projection_parametrization_identity}, we introduce the operator $\aleph:\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}$  with definition
    \begin{equation}\label{e_6}
    [{\aleph(\phi,g)}](x,t) = \frac{1}{V(x)\cdot\nabla\Psi(x)}(t - \phi(x + g(x,t)V(x)) + \nabla\Psi(x)\cdot V(x)g(x,t) - \Psi(x + g(x,t)V(x)))
  \end{equation}
  for $(x,t)\in W_\varepsilon\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]$ and $(\phi,g)\in\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{X}$. The properties of $V$ from Lemma~\ref{lem on regularization of the unit normal} and the selection of parameters~\eqref{parameter_splurge} are much more conservative than what is required to ensure that $\aleph$ is well-defined, in the sense that all of the compositions in its definition are permissible and it maps into the stated codomain of $\mathcal{X}$. More precisely, it is straightforward to verify that 
  \begin{equation}\label{sup_inf}
    \lVert\aleph(\phi,g)\rVert_{C_{\mathrm{b}}^0(W_\varepsilon\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon])}\leqslant\alpha\lambda,
  \end{equation}
    \begin{equation}
    \lVert\aleph(\phi,g) - \aleph(\phi,\widetilde{g})\rVert_{C_{\mathrm{b}}^0(W_\varepsilon\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon])}\leqslant\alpha\lvg - \widetilde{g}\rVert_{C^0_{\mathrm{b}}(W_\varepsilon\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon])},
  \end{equation}
  and
  \begin{equation}
    \lVert\aleph(\phi,g) - \aleph(\widetilde{\phi},g)\rVert_{C^0_{\mathrm{b}}(W_\varepsilon\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon])}\leqslant(4\beta/3)\lVert\phi - \widetilde{\phi}\rVert_{C^0_{\mathrm{b}}(\overline{U})},
  \end{equation}
  for all $(\phi,g),(\widetilde{\phi},\widetilde{g})\in\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{X}$.

  The contraction mapping principle with parameter thus applies, see, e.g., Theorem C.7 in Irwin~\cite{MR1867353}, and we acquire a Lipschitz continuous mapping
  \begin{equation}\label{e_8}
    \mathbf{f}:\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{X}\text{ such that }\aleph(\phi,\mathbf{f}(\phi)) = \mathbf{f}(\phi),\;\forall\;\phi\in\mathcal{B}.
  \end{equation}
  By unpacking the meaning of this fixed point from the definition~\eqref{e_6}, we deduce that precisely the second item holds. 

  Let us now turn our attention to the third item. From~\eqref{e_6} we deduce, for $\phi\in\mathcal{B}$ and $(x,t)\in W_\varepsilon\times[{-\varepsilon,\varepsilon}]$ the estimates
  \begin{equation}
    |[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t)|\leqslant\frac{4\beta}{3}(|\Psi(x) + \phi(x) - t| + (r + \omega(\lambda))|[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t)|)
  \end{equation}
  and
  \begin{equation}
    |[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t)|\geqslant\frac{4}{5\beta}(|\Psi(x) + \phi(x) - t| - (r + \omega(\lambda))|[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t)|)
  \end{equation}
  The two sided bounds claimed in~\eqref{distance to the manifold bound} now follow. As a special consequence of these estimates, we can establish the inclusion asserted within the first item. Indeed, if $x_0\in M$ is such that $|x - x_0|\leqslant\varepsilon$, then
  \begin{equation}
    |x + V(x)[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t) - x_0|\leqslant\varepsilon + 2\beta|\Psi(x) + \phi(x) - t|<2^{-37}\ell
  \end{equation}
  Thus~\eqref{occasionally_useful} holds.

  Next, we justify the fourth item. For any $\phi\in\mathcal{B}$ and $(x,t),(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})\in W_\varepsilon\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]$ satisfying $|x - \widetilde{x}|\leqslant\lambda/2$ the line segment connecting $G_\phi(x,t)$ and $G_\phi(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})$ (where $G_\phi$ is defined in~\eqref{occasionally_useful}) is contained within the interior of $W_{\ell}$. Thus we can use the fundamental theorem of calculus when examining the difference between~\eqref{projection_parametrization_identity} evaluated at $(x,t)$ and $(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})$. The result of doing so is the identity
  \begin{multline}\label{key_difference_identity}
    t - \widetilde{t} = ((x - \widetilde{x}) + (V(x) - V(\widetilde{x}))[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t))\cdot\int_0^1\nabla(\Psi + \phi)(\tau G_\phi(x,t) + (1 - \tau)G_\phi(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t}))\;\mathrm{d}\tau\\
    +([{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t) - [{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t}))V(\widetilde{x})\cdot\Big(\int_0^1\nabla(\Psi + \phi)(\tau G_\phi(x,t) + (1 - \tau)G_\phi(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t}))\;\mathrm{d}\tau\Big) - \nabla\Psi(G_\phi(x,t))\\
    +([{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t) - [{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t}))V(\widetilde{x})\cdot\nabla\Psi(G_\phi(x,t)).
  \end{multline}
  In turn, we get the estimates
  \begin{multline}\label{estimates_in_turn}
    |[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t) - [{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})|\leqslant(4\beta/3)(\omega(\lambda) + r)|[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t) - [{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})| \\+ \beta(5/3 + 4r/3)(1 + n\alpha\lambda/\ell)|x - \widetilde{x}| + (4\beta/3)|t - \widetilde{t}|
  \end{multline}
  and hence we get the Lipschitz bound~\eqref{initial_lip_est}.
  
  We may exploit identity~\eqref{key_difference_identity} via a standard argument implementing difference quotients to deduce that $\mathbf{f}(\phi)$ is actually differentiable at every point in $W_\varepsilon\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]$. The Lipschitz bounds~\eqref{initial_lip_est} directly lead to bounds on the differential. Furthermore, the mapping of $\phi\mapsto D[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}]$ is observed to be continuous. This completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- lem-13: body_md -->
\label{lem on estimates on almost tangent vectors}
    Suppose that $(x,t),(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})\in W_\varepsilon\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]$ satisfy $|x - \widetilde{x}|\leqslant\lambda/2$. For any $z\in W_\ell$ satisfying $|z - \widetilde{x}|\leqslant\lambda$ and any $\phi\in\mathcal{B}$ we have the estimate
    \begin{equation}\label{the almost tangent vector estimate}
        |\nabla(\Psi+\phi)(z)\cdot(x - \widetilde{x})|\leqslant(\omega(2\lambda) + 2r)|x - \widetilde{x}| + |(\Psi + \phi)(x) - (\Psi + \phi)(\widetilde{x})|.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lem-13 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
The argument is very simple. From the fundamental theorem of calculus, we have the equality
    \begin{equation}
       \nabla(\Psi + \phi)(z)\cdot(x - \widetilde{x}) = (\Psi + \phi)(x) - (\Psi + \phi)(\widetilde{x}) + (x - \widetilde{x})\cdot\int_0^1(\nabla(\Psi + \phi)(z) - \nabla(\Psi + \phi)(\tau x + (1 - \tau)\widetilde{x}))\;\mathrm{d}\tau.
    \end{equation}
    Taking the length and using $|z-(\tau x+(1-\tau)\widetilde{x})|\leqslant2\lambda$ gives~\eqref{the almost tangent vector estimate}.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- lem-14: body_md -->
\label{lem on bilipschitz bounds}
  Regarding the map $G$ defined in the first item of Lemma~\ref{lem on regularized distance to the manifold}, the following hold for all $\phi\in\mathcal{B}$, $(x,t),(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})\in W_\varepsilon\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]$ that satisfy $|x - \widetilde{x}|\leqslant\lambda/2$.
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item We have the Lipschitz bound
    \begin{equation}\label{LIP_1}
      \frac{1}{2\beta}|t - \widetilde{t}|\leqslant|G_\phi(x,t) - G_\phi(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})|\leqslant8\beta|x - \widetilde{x},t - \widetilde{t}|.
    \end{equation}
    \item If there exists $y,\widetilde{y}\in W_\varepsilon$, $\psi\in\mathcal{B}$ with
    \begin{equation}\label{LIPO}
      x - \widetilde{x} = y - \widetilde{y},\;|\widetilde{x} - \widetilde{y}| + |x - \widetilde{x}|\leqslant\lambda/2,\;(\Psi + \psi)(y) = (\Psi + \psi)(\widetilde{y}),
    \end{equation}
    then we have the reverse Lipschitz bound
    \begin{equation}\label{LIP_2}
      |x - \widetilde{x},t - \widetilde{t}|\leqslant 8\beta^2|G_\phi(x,t) - G_\phi(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})|.
    \end{equation}
  \end{enumerate}
<!-- end lem-14 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
The right-hand estimate of the first item is a direct consequence of the Lipschitz bounds from the second item of Lemma~\ref{lem on regularization of the unit normal} and the fourth item of Lemma~\ref{lem on regularized distance to the manifold}.

    We turn our attention to the left hand inequality of~\eqref{LIP_1}. From identity~\eqref{projection_parametrization_identity}, we acquire that
    \begin{equation}\label{weve_been_here}
      t - \widetilde{t} = (\Psi + \phi)\circ G_\phi(x,t)  - (\Psi + \phi)\circ G_\phi(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t}).
    \end{equation}
    As we have noted in the derivation of equation~\eqref{key_difference_identity}, the line segment connecting $G_\phi(x,t)$ and $G_\phi(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})$ is contained entirely within the interior of $W_\ell$. Hence, the fundamental theorem of calculus takes us from identity~\eqref{weve_been_here} to the bound asserted on the left of~\eqref{LIP_1}.

    The second item is more subtle. We begin with the bound
    \begin{multline}\label{marco}
      |G_\phi(x,t) - G_\phi(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})|\geqslant|x - \widetilde{x}| - |[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t)||V(x) - V(\widetilde{x})| - |[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t) - [{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})|\\
      \geqslant(1 - \alpha^2)|x - \widetilde{x}| - |[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t) - [{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})|.
    \end{multline}
    To continue, we require a more precise Lipschitz estimate on $\mathbf{f}(\phi)$. We examine identity~\eqref{key_difference_identity} more carefully. The following more precise version of~\eqref{estimates_in_turn} holds:
    \begin{multline}\label{estimates_in_turn_again}
      |[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t) - [{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})|\leqslant\frac{4\beta}{3}(\omega(\lambda) + r)|[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t) - [{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})| +\frac{4\beta}{3}\Big(1 + \frac{n\alpha\lambda}{\ell}\Big)|x - \widetilde{x}| \\+ \frac{4\beta}{3}|t - \widetilde{t}| + \frac{4\beta}{3}\Big|(x - \widetilde{x})\cdot\int_0^1\nabla(\Psi + \phi)(\tau G_\phi(x,t) + (1 - \tau)G_\phi(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t}))\;\mathrm{d}\tau\Big|.
    \end{multline}
    To estimate the final term above we now use the hypotheses~\eqref{LIPO} in conjunction with Lemma~\ref{lem on estimates on almost tangent vectors}. For every $0\leqslant\tau\leqslant1$, the point
    \begin{equation}
      z_\tau=\tau G_\phi(x,t) + (1 - \tau)G_\phi(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})
    \end{equation}
    belongs to $W_\ell$ and satisfies $|z_\tau-\widetilde{y}|\leqslant\lambda$. We may therefore apply the lemma to $y,\widetilde{y},\psi$, and then estimate the difference between $\nabla\phi$ and $\nabla\psi$. The result is
    \begin{equation}\label{key_almost_tangent}
      \frac{4\beta}{3}\Big|(x - \widetilde{x})\cdot\int_0^1\nabla(\Psi + \phi)(z_\tau)\;\mathrm{d}\tau\Big|\leqslant\frac{4\beta}{3}(\omega(2\lambda) + 4r)|x - \widetilde{x}|.
    \end{equation}
    Notice that the term corresponding to the final right hand side contribution of~\eqref{the almost tangent vector estimate} vanishes identically. Since $\omega(2\lambda)\leqslant\alpha^2$, $\lambda\leqslant\alpha^2$, and $r\leqslant\alpha^9$, the same absorption in estimates~\eqref{estimates_in_turn_again} and~\eqref{key_almost_tangent} gives us the bound
    \begin{equation}\label{aguas_de}
      |[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t) - [{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})|\leqslant2\alpha|x - \widetilde{x}| + 2\beta|t - \widetilde{t}|
    \end{equation}

    The combination of~\eqref{aguas_de}, \eqref{marco}, and the left-hand bound of~\eqref{LIP_1} gives
    \begin{equation}
      |t-\widetilde{t}|\leqslant2\beta|G_\phi(x,t)-G_\phi(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})|
    \end{equation}
    and
    \begin{equation}
      (1-2\alpha-\alpha^2)|x-\widetilde{x}|\leqslant(1+4\beta^2)|G_\phi(x,t)-G_\phi(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})|.
    \end{equation}
    Since $\beta\geqslant1$ and $\alpha\leqslant2^{-10}$, these inequalities yield the reverse Lipschitz inequality~\eqref{LIP_2} with the stated factor $8\beta^2$.
<!-- end proof-31 -->
<!-- lem-15: body_md -->
\label{lem on projection properties}
  Define the class $C^1$ map
  \begin{equation}\label{proj_defn}
    \Pi:W_\varepsilon\to W_{2^{-37}\ell}\text{ via }\Pi(x) = x + V(x)[{\mathbf{f}(0)}](x,0)\text{ for all }x\in W_\varepsilon.
  \end{equation}
  The following hold.
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item For all $x\in W_\varepsilon$ we have that $\Psi\circ\Pi(x) = 0$ and hence $\Pi(x)\in M$.
    \item If $x\in M$, then $\Pi(x) = x$.
    \item If $x,\widetilde{x}\in W_\varepsilon$ satisfy $|x - \widetilde{x}|\leqslant\lambda/2$, then we have the estimate
    \begin{equation}\label{the projection estimate_}
      |x - \Pi(\widetilde{x})|\leqslant 7\beta|x - \widetilde{x}| + 2\beta|\Psi(x)|.
    \end{equation}
  \end{enumerate}
<!-- end lem-15 -->
<!-- proof-32: body_md -->
The first item is immediate from the second item of Lemma~\ref{lem on regularized distance to the manifold} and the definition of $M$~\eqref{this is our base manifold}. 

  For the second item, we note that if $x\in M$, then $\Psi(x) = 0$ and so the third item of Lemma~\ref{lem on regularized distance to the manifold} implies that $[{\mathbf{f}(0)}](x,0) = 0$. Hence $\Pi(x) = x$ directly from the definition~\eqref{proj_defn}.

  Finally, to prove the third item we begin by writing
  \begin{equation}
    x - \Pi(\widetilde{x}) = x - \widetilde{x} - V(\widetilde{x})([{\mathbf{f}(0)}](\widetilde{x},0) - [{\mathbf{f}(0)}](x,0)) - V(\widetilde{x})[{\mathbf{f}(0)}](x,0).
  \end{equation}
  We then measure the length and use the estimates from the third and fourth items of Lemma~\ref{lem on regularized distance to the manifold} to deduce the bound~\eqref{the projection estimate_}.
<!-- end proof-32 -->
<!-- thm-4: body_md -->
\label{thm on stable local level set coordinates}
  Consider the class $C^1$ maps
  \begin{equation}\label{The coordinate maps}
    \Theta_\phi:M\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]\to W_{2^{-37}\ell}\text{ given via the restriction }\Theta_\phi(m,t) = G_\phi(m,t)
  \end{equation}
  and defined for $\phi\in\mathcal{B}$, $(m,t)\in M\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]$, where $G$ is from the first item of Lemma~\ref{lem on regularized distance to the manifold}. The following hold.
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item The maps $\Theta_\phi$ are injective; in fact, for all $\phi\in\mathcal{B}$ and all $(m,t),(\widetilde{m},\widetilde{t})\in M\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]$ we have the two-sided Lipschitz bound
    \begin{equation}\label{two_sided_lipschitz}
      \frac{1}{8\beta^2}|m - \widetilde{m},t - \widetilde{t}|\leqslant|\Theta_\phi(m,t) - \Theta_\phi(\widetilde{m},\widetilde{t})|\leqslant 8\beta|m - \widetilde{m},t - \widetilde{t}|.
    \end{equation}
    \item The maps $\Theta_\phi$ are uniformly locally surjective in the sense that 
    \begin{equation}\label{local_surjectivity}
      \forall\;\phi\in\mathcal{B},\;\Theta_\phi(M\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon])\supseteq W_{(\alpha\lambda)^3}.
    \end{equation}
    \item Whenever $\phi\in\mathcal{B}$ and $t\in\mathbb{R}$ satisfy $\lVert\phi\rVert_{C^{0,1}(\overline{U})}\leqslant\alpha(\alpha\lambda)^3$ and $|t|\leqslant\alpha(\alpha\lambda)^3$ we have the inclusion
    \begin{equation}\label{level_set_covering}
      \{{x\in W_\varepsilon\;:\;\Psi(x) + \phi(x) = t}\}\subset W_{(\alpha\lambda)^3}.
    \end{equation}
    \item The mapping $\mathcal{B}\ni\widetilde{\phi}\mapsto\Theta_{\widetilde{\phi}}\in C^1_{\mathrm{b}}(M\times[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon];\mathbb{R}^n)$ is continuous.
  \end{enumerate}
<!-- end thm-4 -->
<!-- proof-33: body_md -->
We begin by remarking that the fourth item is a trivial consequence of the fifth item of Lemma~\ref{lem on regularized distance to the manifold}.

  Let us continue with the proof of the first item. The two-sided Lipschitz bound~\eqref{two_sided_lipschitz} was already established under the additional assumption that $|m - \widetilde{m}|\leqslant\lambda/2$, see Lemma~\ref{lem on bilipschitz bounds}. So we consider the case that $|m - \widetilde{m}|>\lambda/2$. For the upper bound, we note that the definition of $\Theta_\phi$ and the uniform bound~\eqref{sup_inf} give: 
  \begin{equation}
    |\Theta_\phi(m,t) - \Theta_\phi(\widetilde{m},\widetilde{t})|\leqslant|m - \widetilde{m}| + 2\alpha\lambda\leqslant(1 + 4\alpha)|m - \widetilde{m}|.
  \end{equation}
  The same strategy gives the lower bound 
  \begin{equation}
    |\Theta_\phi(m,t) - \Theta_\phi(\widetilde{m},\widetilde{t})|\geqslant|m - \widetilde{m}| - 2\alpha\lambda\geqslant(1 - 4(\alpha + \alpha^2))|m - \widetilde{m}| + |t - \widetilde{t}|.
  \end{equation}

  The second item requires a somewhat delicate argument. Let $\phi\in\mathcal{B}$ and let $y\in W_{(\alpha\lambda)^3}$. By definition~\eqref{tub_nei}, there exists $x_0\in M$ such that $|x_0 - y|\leqslant(\alpha\lambda)^3$. Let us set $t = (\Psi + \phi)(y)$. We get the estimate 
  \begin{equation}\label{estimate_on_t}
    |t|\leqslant r + |\Psi(y) - \Psi(x_0)|\leqslant(1 + 5\beta/4)(\alpha\lambda)^3<\varepsilon.
  \end{equation}
  Now define the ball and the map
  \begin{equation}\label{map_to_be_fixed}
    B = \{{x\in\mathbb{R}^n\;:\;|x - x_0|\leqslant(\alpha\lambda)^2}\}\subset W_{(\alpha\lambda)^2}\text{ and }F:B\to\mathbb{R}^n,\;F(x) = \Pi(y - V(\Pi(x))[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\Pi(x),t))
  \end{equation}
  for all $x\in B$. Recall that the map $\Pi$ appearing above is the projection operator introduced in Lemma~\ref{lem on projection properties}. To check that $F$ is indeed well-defined, we need that for all $x\in B$ the argument of the outermost $\Pi$ belongs to its domain $W_\varepsilon$. By using the third item of Lemma~\ref{lem on regularized distance to the manifold} and the intermediate bound of~\eqref{estimate_on_t}, we get the estimate
  \begin{equation}
    |y - V(\Pi(x))[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\Pi(x),t) - x_0|\leqslant(\alpha\lambda)^3 + 2\beta((\alpha\lambda)^3 + |t|)\leqslant(\alpha\lambda)^3(1 + 2\beta(2 + 5\beta/4))<\varepsilon.
  \end{equation}
  So indeed $F$ is well-defined; this map is also continuous. We claim now that $F(B)\subseteq B$. To estimate the difference between $F(x)$ and $x_0$ for an arbitrary $x\in B$, we employ first the third item of Lemma~\ref{lem on projection properties}, thus
  \begin{equation}
    |x_0 - F(x)|\leqslant 7\beta|x_0 - y + V(\Pi(x))[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\Pi(x),t)|.
  \end{equation}
  Next, we use again the third item of Lemma~\ref{lem on regularized distance to the manifold} and the intermediate bound of~\eqref{estimate_on_t}, therefore 
  \begin{equation}
    |x_0 - F(x)|\leqslant 7\beta(\alpha\lambda)^3 + 14\beta^2(1 + 5\beta/4)(\alpha\lambda)^3\leqslant2^{-20}(\alpha\lambda)^2.
  \end{equation}

  Brouwer's fixed point theorem thus applies for the map $F$. We therefore deduce the existence of $x\in B$ such that $x = F(x)$. From the definition of $F$~\eqref{map_to_be_fixed} and the first item of Lemma~\ref{lem on projection properties}, we see that the fixed point $x$ satisfies the inclusion $x\in M$. In turn, the second item of the aforementioned result implies that $x = \Pi(x)$. Thus, upon unpacking the fixed point identity $x = F(x)$ we learn that 
  \begin{equation}\label{step_on}
    \Pi(x) = \Pi(y - V(x)[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t)).
  \end{equation}
  We would like to conclude that the arguments of $\Pi$ in~\eqref{step_on} are also equal. The second item of Lemma~\ref{lem on bilipschitz bounds} is the key to doing so. Let us set
  \begin{equation}
    w = x,\;\widetilde{w} = y - V(x)[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t),\;z = x + V(x)[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t),\;\widetilde{z} = y.
  \end{equation}
  Then 
  \begin{equation}
    w - \widetilde{w} = z - \widetilde{z},\;|\widetilde{w} - \widetilde{z}| + |w - \widetilde{w}|\leqslant\lambda/2,\;(\Psi + \phi)(z) = t = (\Psi + \phi)(\widetilde{z}).
  \end{equation}
  Thus the reverse Lipschitz bound~\eqref{LIP_2} applies and we find that 
  \begin{equation}
    |x - y + V(x)[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](x,t)| = |w - \widetilde{w}|\leqslant8\beta^2|\Pi(w) - \Pi(\widetilde{w})| = 0\text{ and hence }y = \Theta_\phi(x,t).
  \end{equation}

  Finally, we turn our attention to the third item. We first claim that for all $x\in W_\varepsilon$ we have that 
  \begin{equation}\label{kitty_}
    \mathrm{dist}(x,M)\leqslant2\beta|\Psi(x)|.
  \end{equation}
  To prove~\eqref{kitty_}, we begin by letting $x\in W_\varepsilon$ and selecting $x_0\in M$ such that $|x - x_0| = \mathrm{dist}(x,M)$. The class $C^1$ function $M\ni y\mapsto|x - y|^2\in\mathbb{R}$ has a minimum at $y = x_0$. Thus the differential vanishes for all directions belonging to the tangent space at $x_0$. Hence:
  \begin{equation}
    (x - x_0)\cdot T = 0\text{ for all }T\in\mathbb{R}^n\text{ such that }T\cdot\nabla\Psi(x_0) = 0
  \end{equation}
  and so we get equality in Cauchy-Schwarz:
  \begin{equation}\label{reverse_cauchy_schwarz}
    |(x - x_0)\cdot\nabla\Psi(x_0)| = |x - x_0||\nabla\Psi(x_0)|.
  \end{equation}
  Now, from the fundamental theorem of calculus and the fact that $\Psi(x_0) = 0$, we get the bound
  \begin{equation}\label{FTC_AGAIN}
    |\Psi(x)| \geqslant |\nabla\Psi(x_0)\cdot(x - x_0)| - \omega(\lambda)|x - x_0|.
  \end{equation}
  Inequality~\eqref{kitty_} follows by combining~\eqref{reverse_cauchy_schwarz} with~\eqref{FTC_AGAIN}.

  Now if $x\in W_\varepsilon$ is such that $\Psi(x) + \phi(x) = t$ with $\lVert\phi\rVert_{C^{0,1}(\overline{U})}\leqslant\alpha(\alpha\lambda)^3$ and $|t|\leqslant\alpha(\alpha\lambda)^3$, then~\eqref{kitty_} directly informs us that $\mathrm{dist}(x,M)\leqslant(\alpha\lambda)^3$. The proof is complete.
<!-- end proof-33 -->
<!-- lem-16: body_md -->
\label{lem on reverse Lipschitz bound with projection}
  Suppose that $\hat{m}\in M$ and $\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$ satisfy $\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})\neq 0$. Choose $0<\kappa\leqslant\lambda/4$ so small that
  \begin{equation}\label{definition of the parameter kappa}
    \omega(2\kappa)\leqslant\frac{1}{2^{10}\beta}\frac{|\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|}{\max\{{1,|\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|}\}}.
  \end{equation}

  Then for all $m,\widetilde{m}\in M$, $\phi\in\mathcal{B}$, and $t\in[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]$ satisfying
  \begin{equation}\label{wave_is_on_its_way}
    \max\{{|m - \hat{m}|,|\widetilde{m} - \hat{m}|}\}\leqslant\kappa,\;\max\{{\lVert\phi\rVert_{C^{0,1}(\overline{U})},|t|}\}\leqslant\min\Big\{{\frac{1}{2^{10}}\frac{|\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|}{|\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|}\frac{\ell}{n\beta},\frac{\kappa}{8\beta}}\Big\}
  \end{equation}
  the estimate
  \begin{equation}\label{rev_lip_proj}
    |m - \widetilde{m}|\leqslant4\frac{|\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|}{|\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|}|\mathbb{P}(\Theta_\phi(m,t) - \Theta_\phi(\widetilde{m},t))|.
  \end{equation}
  holds with $\mathbb{P} = I - \nu\otimes\nu$.
<!-- end lem-16 -->
<!-- proof-34: body_md -->
We begin by deriving the claimed estimate~\eqref{rev_lip_proj} in the case that $t = 0$ and $\phi = 0$. In this case, the operator $\Theta$ collapses to the identity on $M$, see Lemma~\ref{lem on projection properties}. Fix $m,\widetilde{m}\in \overline{B(\hat{m},\kappa)}\cap M$. By arguing as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem on estimates on almost tangent vectors}, we estimate
  \begin{equation}\label{the_almost_tangent_estimate_redux}
    |(m - \widetilde{m})\cdot\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|\leqslant\omega(\kappa)|m - \widetilde{m}|.
  \end{equation}
  Combining~\eqref{the_almost_tangent_estimate_redux} with the decomposition $m - \widetilde{m} = \nu(m - \widetilde{m})\cdot\nu + \mathbb{P}(m - \widetilde{m})$ shows that
  \begin{equation}
    |\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})||\nu\cdot(m - \widetilde{m})|\leqslant\omega(\kappa)|m - \widetilde{m}| + |\mathbb{P}(m - \widetilde{m})\cdot\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|.
  \end{equation}
  Hence from~\eqref{definition of the parameter kappa}, the triangle inequality, and
  $1+|\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|/|\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|\leqslant2|\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|/|\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|$, we obtain
  \begin{equation}\label{estimate in the special case}
    |m - \widetilde{m}|\leqslant3\frac{|\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|}{|\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|}|\mathbb{P}(m - \widetilde{m})|.
  \end{equation}
  
  Now suppose that $\phi\in\mathcal{B}$ and $t\in[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]$. From the definition of $\Theta$~\eqref{The coordinate maps}, it follows that 
  \begin{multline}\label{__FiNaL__}
    |\mathbb{P}(m - \widetilde{m})|\leqslant|\mathbb{P}(\Theta_\phi(m,t) - \Theta_\phi(\widetilde{m},t))| + |\mathbb{P}(V(m)[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](m,t) - V(\widetilde{m})[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\widetilde{m},t))|\\
    \leqslant|\mathbb{P}(\Theta_\phi(m,t) - \Theta_\phi(\widetilde{m},t))| + (n/\ell)|m - \widetilde{m}||[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](m,t)| + |[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](m,t) - [{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\widetilde{m},t)|.
  \end{multline}
  Then, from the third item of Lemma~\ref{lem on regularized distance to the manifold} and the hypotheses~\eqref{wave_is_on_its_way}, we find
  \begin{equation}\label{_not_done_yet_}
    \frac{n}{\ell}|[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](m,t)|\leqslant2\frac{n\beta}{\ell}|\phi(m) - t|\leqslant\frac{1}{24}\frac{|\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|}{|\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|}.
  \end{equation}

  To estimate the difference $[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](m,t) - [{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\widetilde{m},t)$, we note first that $|m-\widetilde{m}|\leqslant2\kappa\leqslant\lambda/2$. We may therefore examine identity~\eqref{key_difference_identity} as in the derivation of estimate~\eqref{estimates_in_turn_again}. One finds that
  \begin{equation}\label{e_1__}
    |[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](m,t) - [{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\widetilde{m},t)|\leqslant4\beta\lVert\phi\rVert_{C^{0,1}(\overline{U})}|m - \widetilde{m}| + 2\beta\Big|(m - \widetilde{m})\cdot\int_0^1\nabla\Psi(\tau\Theta_\phi(m,t) + (1 - \tau)\Theta_\phi(\widetilde{m},t))\;\mathrm{d}\tau\Big|.
  \end{equation}
  The final term above can then be estimated as
  \begin{equation}\label{e_2__}
    \Big|(m - \widetilde{m})\cdot\int_0^1\nabla\Psi(\tau\Theta_\phi(m,t) + (1 - \tau)\Theta_\phi(\widetilde{m},t))\;\mathrm{d}\tau\Big|\leqslant|m - \widetilde{m}|\omega(2\beta(|t| + \lVert\phi\rVert_{C^{0,1}(\overline{U})})).
  \end{equation}
  Indeed, subtract the same integral along the segment joining $m$ and $\widetilde{m}$, whose pairing with $m-\widetilde{m}$ equals $\Psi(m)-\Psi(\widetilde{m})=0$, and then use the third item of Lemma~\ref{lem on regularized distance to the manifold}.

  The hypotheses imply
  \begin{equation}
    2\beta(|t|+\lVert\phi\rVert_{C^{0,1}(\overline U)})\leqslant\kappa/2.
  \end{equation}
  Moreover, the two coefficients on the right-hand side of~\eqref{e_1__} are bounded respectively by
  \begin{equation}
    2^{-8}\frac{|\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi(\hat m)|}{|\nabla\Psi(\hat m)|}
    \text{ and }
    2^{-9}\frac{|\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi(\hat m)|}{|\nabla\Psi(\hat m)|}.
  \end{equation}
  Thus~\eqref{definition of the parameter kappa} and~\eqref{wave_is_on_its_way} give
  \begin{equation}\label{good_lippy_bound}
    |[{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](m,t) - [{\mathbf{f}(\phi)}](\widetilde{m},t)|\leqslant\frac{1}{24}\frac{|\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|}{|\nabla\Psi(\hat{m})|}|m - \widetilde{m}|.
  \end{equation}

  Combining estimates~\eqref{__FiNaL__}, \eqref{_not_done_yet_}, and~\eqref{good_lippy_bound} gives
  \begin{equation}
    |\mathbb{P}(m-\widetilde{m})|\leqslant|\mathbb{P}(\Theta_\phi(m,t)-\Theta_\phi(\widetilde{m},t))|+\frac1{12}\frac{|\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi(\hat m)|}{|\nabla\Psi(\hat m)|}|m-\widetilde{m}|.
  \end{equation}
  Inserting this estimate into~\eqref{estimate in the special case} yields
  \begin{equation}
    |m-\widetilde{m}|\leqslant3\frac{|\nabla\Psi(\hat m)|}{|\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi(\hat m)|}|\mathbb{P}(\Theta_\phi(m,t)-\Theta_\phi(\widetilde{m},t))|+\frac14|m-\widetilde{m}|,
  \end{equation}
  and the absorption of the final term proves~\eqref{rev_lip_proj}.
<!-- end proof-34 -->
<!-- lem-17: body_md -->
\label{lem on density estimates on a fixed manifold}
  Suppose that $1\leqslant k<n$ and $M\subset \mathbb{R}^n$ is a compact $k$-dimensional manifold of class $C^1$. Then, there exists a constant $C\in\mathbb{R}^+$ such that for all $x\in\mathbb{R}^n$ and all $r\in\mathbb{R}^+$ we have the estimate
  \begin{equation}\label{density_estimate_1}
    \mathcal{H}^k(M\cap B(x,r))\leqslant Cr^k
  \end{equation}
  where $\mathcal{H}^k$ denotes the $k$-dimensional Hausdorff measure.
<!-- end lem-17 -->
<!-- proof-35: body_md -->
By the local graph representation for class $C^1$ submanifolds, see, e.g., Section 3.1 in Federer~~\cite{MR257325}, the manifold $M$ is locally diffeomorphic to its tangent spaces. More precisely, for each $m\in M$, upon letting $A_m = \{{m + v\;:\;v\in T_mM}\}$, there exists $0<\rho_m<1$ and a $C^1$ diffeomorphism
  \begin{equation}
    f_m:B(m,\rho_m)\to f_m(B(m,\rho_m))\subset\mathbb{R}^n
  \end{equation}
  satisfying
  \begin{equation}
    \max\{{\lVert\nabla f_m\rVert_{L^\infty(B(m,\rho_m))},\lVert\nabla f_m^{-1}\rVert_{L^\infty(f_m(B(m,\rho_m)))}}\}\leqslant 2
  \end{equation}
  and
  \begin{equation}\label{flattening_identity}
    B(m,\rho_m/2)\cap M = B(m,\rho_m/2)\cap f_m^{-1}(A_m).
  \end{equation}

  By compactness of $M$, there exists $N\in\mathbb{N}^+$ and $\{{m_j}\}_{j=1}^N\subset M$ such that 
  \begin{equation}
    M \subset \bigcup_{j=1}^N B(m_j,\rho_{j})\text{ where we have defined }\rho_j = \frac14\rho_{m_j}.
  \end{equation}

  Now suppose that $x\in\mathbb{R}^n$ and $0<r\in\mathbb{R}^+$. Thanks to identity~\eqref{flattening_identity} and Theorem 2.8 in Evans and Gariepy~\cite{MR3409135} we have the estimate
  \begin{equation}\label{eq_1}
    \mathcal{H}^k(M\cap B(x,r))\leqslant\sum_{j=1}^N\mathcal{H}^k(M\cap B(m_j,\rho_j)\cap B(x,r) )\leqslant 2^k\sum_{j=1}^N\mathcal{H}^k(A_{j}\cap f_j(B(m_j,\rho_j)\cap B(x,r))),
  \end{equation}
  in which we have abbreviated $f_j = f_{m_j}$ and $A_{j} = A_{m_j}$. The affine subspace $A_j$ is isometrically isomorphic to $\mathbb{R}^k$. Therefore, by the coincidence of Hausdorff and Lebesgue measures on $\mathbb{R}^k$ and the isodiametric inequality (see, e.g., Theorems 2.4 and 2.5 in~\cite{MR3409135}) we see that for each $j\in\{{1,\dots,N}\}$ it holds that
  \begin{equation}\label{eq_2}
    \mathrm{diam}(A_j\cap f_j(B(m_j,\rho_j)\cap B(x,r)))\leqslant 4r\text{ and }\mathcal{H}^k(A_{j}\cap f_j(B(m_j,\rho_j)\cap B(x,r)))\leqslant\frac{(2\sqrt{\pi}r)^k}{\Upgamma(k/2 + 1)},
  \end{equation}
  where $\Upgamma$ denotes Euler's gamma function. The desired estimate~\eqref{density_estimate_1} now follows by combining~\eqref{eq_1} with~\eqref{eq_2} and taking $C = N(4\sqrt{\pi})^k/\Upgamma(k/2 + 1)$.
<!-- end proof-35 -->
<!-- thm-5: body_md -->
\label{thm on uniform density estimates on families of hypersurfaces}
  Let $\mathbb{R}\ni q\geqslant0$, $\mathbb{N}\ni n\geqslant 2$, $\varnothing\neq U\subset\mathbb{R}^n$ be open and bounded, $\Psi\in C^1_{\mathrm{b}}(\overline{U})$, and $a,b\in\mathbb{R}$ with $a\leqslant b$. Assume that 
  \begin{equation}\label{hypotheses_are_here}
    E = \{{x\in\overline{U}\;:\;\Psi(x)\in[a,b]}\},\;E\Subset U,\;\min_E|\nabla\Psi|>0.
  \end{equation}
  Then, there exist constants $C,\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}^+$ such that the following hold for all $a - \varepsilon \leqslant t\leqslant b+\varepsilon$, $\phi\in C^1(\overline{U})$ satisfying $\lVert\phi\rVert_{C^{0,1}(\overline{U})}\leqslant \varepsilon$, and $\rho\in\mathbb{R}^+$.
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item We have the inclusions
    \begin{equation}\label{INC_1}
      M(\phi,t) = \{{x\in U\;:\;\Psi(x) + \phi(x) = t}\}\subseteq\{{x\in U\;:\;a-2\varepsilon\leqslant\Psi(x)\leqslant b+2\varepsilon}\}\Subset U.
    \end{equation}
    \item If $M(\phi,t)\neq\varnothing$, we have the uniform positivity estimate
    \begin{equation}\label{POS_1}
      \min\{{|\nabla\Psi(x) + \nabla\phi(x)|\;:\;x\in M(\phi,t)}\}\geqslant1/C.
    \end{equation}
    Moreover, if $M(0,s)\neq\varnothing$ for all $a\leqslant s\leqslant b$, then, for $t\in[a,b]$,
    \begin{equation}\label{POS_2}
      \mathcal{H}^{n-1}(M(\phi,t))\geqslant1/C.
    \end{equation}
    \item We have the uniform density estimate
    \begin{equation}\label{EST_1}
      \sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^n}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(M(\phi,t)\cap B(x,\rho))\leqslant C\rho^{n-1}.
    \end{equation}
    \item If $0\leqslant q<n-1$, we have the estimate
    \begin{equation}\label{EST_2}
      \sup_{x\in U}\int_{M(\phi,t)\cap B(x,\rho)}|\lambda^q(x - z)|\;\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(z)\leqslant C
      \begin{cases}
      \rho^{n- q -1}&\text{if }q>0,\\
      \rho^{n-1}(1+|\log\rho|)&\text{if }q = 0.
      \end{cases}
    \end{equation}
    \item If $n-1\leqslant q$, we have the estimate
    \begin{equation}\label{EST_3}
      \sup_{x\in U}\int_{M(\phi,t)\setminus B(x,\rho)}|\lambda^q(x - z)|\;\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(z)\leqslant C\begin{cases}
      \rho^{n-q-1}&\text{if }q>n-1,\\
      1 + |\log|\rho||&\text{if }q = n-1.
      \end{cases}
    \end{equation}
  \end{enumerate}
<!-- end thm-5 -->
<!-- proof-36: body_md -->
By using that $E\Subset U$ and that $\min_{E}|\nabla\Psi|>0$, we may select $\hat{a},\hat{b}\in\mathbb{R}$ such that $\hat{a}<a$, $b<\hat{b}$, and
  \begin{equation}\label{fat_set}
    \hat{E} = \{{x\in U\;:\;\Psi(x)\in[\hat{a},\hat{b}]}\}\Subset U,\text{ and }\min\{{|\nabla\Psi(x)|\;:\;x\in\hat{E}}\}>0.
  \end{equation}

  Set $\mathcal{K}=\Psi(\hat E)$, which is a compact subset of $[\hat a,\hat b]$. Now, for each $s\in\mathcal{K}$ consider the map $\Psi_s\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^1(\overline{U})$ defined via $\Psi_s(x) = \Psi(x) - s$; set also $M_s = \{{x\in U\;:\;\Psi_s(x) = 0}\}$. By the definition of $\mathcal K$, each $M_s$ is nonempty. The hypotheses~\eqref{hypotheses_are_here} ensure that Theorem~\ref{thm on stable local level set coordinates} is applicable to each $\Psi_s$. By carefully unpacking the conclusions of this result, we obtain positive constants $C_s, r_s, \varepsilon_s, \widetilde{\varepsilon}_s, \hat{\varepsilon}_s\in\mathbb{R}^+$ such that
  \begin{equation}\label{AI_SLOP}
    \varepsilon_s<\widetilde{\varepsilon}_s<\hat{\varepsilon}_s<\mathrm{dist}(\hat{E},\partial U),\;\varepsilon_s + r_s\leqslant\min\{{|a -\hat{a}|,|b - \hat{b}|}\},\;r_s\leqslant\min_{\hat{E}}|\nabla\Psi|/2
  \end{equation}  
  and the following hold.
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item For each $\phi\in C^1_{\mathrm{b}}(\overline{U})$ with $\lVert\phi\rVert_{C^{0,1}(\overline{U})}\leqslant r_s$ there exists a map $\Theta_{\phi}^s:M_s\times[-\hat{\varepsilon}_s,\hat{\varepsilon}_s]\to U$ that is a $C^1$ diffeomorphism onto its image. Moreover, we have the bi-Lipschitz bound
    \begin{equation}\label{bi_lip}
      (1/C_s)|m - \widetilde{m},\tau - \widetilde{\tau}|\leqslant|\Theta^s_\phi(m,\tau) - \Theta^s_\phi(\widetilde{m},\widetilde{\tau})|\leqslant C_s|m - \widetilde{m},\tau - \widetilde{\tau}|
    \end{equation}
    and the level-set parametrization identity
    \begin{equation}\label{lev_par}
      (\Psi_s + \phi)\circ\Theta_\phi^s(m,\tau) = \tau
    \end{equation}
    for all $(m,\tau),(\widetilde{m},\widetilde{\tau})\in M_s\times[-\hat{\varepsilon}_s,\hat{\varepsilon}_s]$.
    \item For each $\tau\in\mathbb{R}$ and $\phi\in C^1_{\mathrm{b}}(\overline{U})$ satisfying $|\tau|\leqslant\varepsilon_s$ and $\lVert\phi\rVert_{C^{0,1}(\overline{U})}\leqslant r_s$ we have that 
    \begin{multline}\label{some_inclusions}
      \{{x\in U\;:\;|\Psi_s(x)|\leqslant2\varepsilon_s}\}\subseteq\{{x\in U\;:\;\mathrm{dist}(x,M_s)\leqslant\hat{\varepsilon}_s}\}\text{ and}\\
      \{{x\in U\;:\;\mathrm{dist}(x,M_s)\leqslant\hat{\varepsilon}_s,\;\Psi_s(x) + \phi(x) = \tau}\}\subseteq\{{x\in U\;:\;\mathrm{dist}(x,M_s)\leqslant\widetilde{\varepsilon}_s}\}\subseteq\Theta^s_\phi(M_s\times[-\varepsilon_s,\hat{\varepsilon}_s])\Subset U.
    \end{multline}
  \end{enumerate}

  The intervals $\{{t\in\mathbb{R}:|t-s|<\varepsilon_s}\}$ form an open cover of $\mathcal K$. Hence there exist a finite set $\varnothing\neq\digamma\subset\mathcal K$ and a number $\varepsilon_0>0$ such that
  \begin{equation}\label{the_covering_condition}
    \{{t\in\mathbb{R}\;:\;\mathrm{dist}(t,\mathcal K)\leqslant\varepsilon_0}\}\subset\bigcup_{s\in\digamma}\{{t\in\mathbb{R}\;:\;|s - t|<\varepsilon_s}\}.
  \end{equation}
  We then instantiate the positive parameter
  \begin{equation}
    \varepsilon = \min\Big\{{\varepsilon_0,\frac{a-\hat a}{3},\frac{\hat b-b}{3},\min\{{\min\{{\varepsilon_s,r_s}\}/3\;:\;s\in\digamma}\}}\Big\}.
  \end{equation}

  We now prove the first item. If $x\in M(\phi,t)$ for some $\phi\in C^1_{\mathrm{b}}(\overline{U})$ with $\lVert\phi\rVert_{C^{0,1}(\overline{U})}\leqslant\varepsilon$ and $t\in[a-\varepsilon,b + \varepsilon]$ then it is clear that $a-2\varepsilon\leqslant\Psi(x)\leqslant b + 2\varepsilon$ and hence $x\in\hat{E}\Subset U$.

  Whenever $M(\phi,t)\neq\varnothing$, the second item's estimate~\eqref{POS_1} is immediate from the rightmost bound of equation~\eqref{AI_SLOP}.

  To justify the third item, we begin by letting $x\in\mathbb{R}^n$ and $\rho\in\mathbb{R}^+$ while fixing $t$ and $\phi$ as above. It suffices to consider the case that $M(\phi,t)\cap B(x,\rho)\neq\varnothing$.
  
  Fix $x_\ast\in M(\phi,t)\cap B(x,\rho)$. The first item gives $x_\ast\in\hat E$, and hence
  $\mathrm{dist}(t,\mathcal K)\leqslant|t-\Psi(x_\ast)|=|\phi(x_\ast)|\leqslant\varepsilon_0$. From the covering condition~\eqref{the_covering_condition}, we deduce the existence of $s\in\digamma$ such that $|s - t|<\varepsilon_s$. Then, $\tau = t - s$ satisfies $|\tau|\leqslant\varepsilon_s$ and hence we can invoke the inclusion~\eqref{some_inclusions}, the identity~\eqref{lev_par}, and the estimate~\eqref{bi_lip} to deduce
  \begin{equation}
    M(\phi,t)\cap B(x,\rho) = \{{\Theta_\phi^s(m,\tau)\;:\;m\in M_s(x,\rho)}\}\text{ where }M_s(x,\rho) = \{{m\in M_s\;:\;\Theta_\phi^s(m,\tau)\in B(x,\rho)}\}.
  \end{equation}
  Now we use Theorem 2.8 in Evans and Gariepy~\cite{MR3409135} in conjunction with the Lipschitz estimate~\eqref{bi_lip} to arrive at the intermediate inequality
  \begin{equation}\label{com_1}
    \mathcal{H}^{n-1}(M(\phi,t)\cap B(x,\rho))\leqslant(C_s)^{n-1}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(M_s(x,\rho)).
  \end{equation}
  
  Next, we again employ the bounds~\eqref{bi_lip} to deduce that 
  \begin{equation}
    m\in M_s(x,\rho)\;\Rightarrow\; m\in B(\hat{m},(2C_s)\rho)\cap M_s,
  \end{equation}
  where $\hat{m}\in M_s(x,\rho)$ is arbitrarily fixed. In turn, Lemma~\ref{lem on density estimates on a fixed manifold} gives us the existence of a finite constant $\widetilde{C}_s$, depending only on the fixed compact hypersurface $M_s$, such that 
  \begin{equation}\label{com_2}
    \mathcal{H}^{n-1}(M_s(x,\rho))\leqslant\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(M_s\cap B(\hat{m},2C_s\rho))\leqslant\widetilde{C}_s(2 C_s\rho)^{n-1}.
  \end{equation}
  Combining~\eqref{com_1} and~\eqref{com_2} gives the desired density estimate~\eqref{EST_1} with the finite constant 
  \begin{equation}
    C = 2^{n-1}\max_{s\in\digamma}\widetilde{C}_s(C_s)^{2n - 2}.
  \end{equation}

  The strategy for the claimed estimate~\eqref{POS_2} is similar, yet simpler. Assume the additional nonemptiness hypothesis and fix $t\in[a,b]$ and $\phi$ as above. Then $M(0,t)\neq\varnothing$, so $t\in\mathcal K$. From condition~\eqref{the_covering_condition}, we are given $s\in\digamma$ such that $|s - t|<\varepsilon_s$; then, $\tau = t - s$ again obeys $|\tau|\leqslant\varepsilon_s$ thus allowing us to employ equations~\eqref{lev_par} and~\eqref{some_inclusions} and deduce 
  \begin{equation}
    M(\phi,t) = \{{\Theta^s_\phi(m,\tau)\;:\;m\in M_s}\}.
  \end{equation}
  Now we use yet again Theorem 2.8 in~\cite{MR3409135} in conjunction with the Lipschitz estimate~\eqref{bi_lip} to estimate
  \begin{equation}
    \mathcal{H}^{n-1}(M_s)\leqslant(C_s)^{n-1}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(M(\phi,t))
  \end{equation}
  and hence 
  \begin{equation}
    \mathcal{H}^{n-1}(M(\phi,t))\geqslant\min\{{(C_s)^{1-n}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(M_s)\;:\;s\in\digamma}\}>0.
  \end{equation}
  Note that $M_s\neq\varnothing$ and hence $\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(M_s)>0$ for all $s\in\digamma$, since $\digamma\subset\mathcal K$.

  To prove the fourth item, we use the third item and dyadic decomposition. For $\ell\in\mathbb{Z}$ and $x\in\mathbb{R}^n$ we let 
  \begin{equation}\label{annulus_region}
    A_\ell(x,\rho) = B(x,2^{-\ell}\rho)\setminus B(x,2^{-\ell-1}\rho),
  \end{equation}
  and expand the integral as a sum over the regions~\eqref{annulus_region}. Upon doing so, we have the estimate
  \begin{equation}
    \int_{M(\phi,t)\cap B(x,\rho)}\frac{1}{|x - z|^q}\;\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(z)\leqslant\rho^{-q}\sum_{\ell=0}^\infty2^{q(\ell+1)}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(M(\phi,t)\cap B(x,2^{-\ell}\rho))\leqslant\frac{2^qC}{1 - 2^{q-n+1}}\rho^{n-q-1}.
  \end{equation}
  This implies the desired bound~\eqref{EST_2} in the case that $q>0$. The case $q = 0$ follows by a nearly identical argument.

  The fifth item is also very similar. If $M(\phi,t)=\varnothing$, there is nothing to prove. Otherwise, let $x\in U$ and set 
  \begin{equation}
    K = \min\{{k\in\mathbb{N}\;:\;M(\phi,t)\subset B(x,2^k\rho)}\}.
  \end{equation}
  By dyadic decomposition again, we have
  \begin{multline}\label{slighty}
    \int_{M(\phi,t)\setminus B(x,\rho)}\frac{1}{|x - z|^q}\;\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(z)\leqslant\rho^{-q}\sum_{\ell=1}^K 2^{q(-\ell + 1)}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(M(\phi,t)\cap B(x,2^{\ell}\rho))\\\leqslant\begin{cases}
      \frac{2^{n-1}C}{1 - 2^{n-1-q}}\rho^{n-q-1}&\text{if }n-1<q,\\
      2^qCK&\text{if }n-1 = q.
    \end{cases}
  \end{multline}
  We obtain the sought-after estimate~\eqref{EST_3} from~\eqref{slighty} by estimating  $K \leqslant 1 + |\log_2(\mathrm{diam}U/\rho)|$.
<!-- end proof-36 -->
<!-- coro-6: body_md -->
\label{bounds for integral operators on thin domains}
  Let $\mathbb{R}\ni q\geqslant0$ satisfy $q<n$, $\mathbb{N}\ni n\geqslant2$, $\varnothing\neq U\subset\mathbb{R}^n$ be open and bounded, $\Psi\in C^1_{\mathrm{b}}(\overline{U})$, and $a,b\in\mathbb{R}$ with $a\leqslant b$. Assume that~\eqref{hypotheses_are_here} holds and let $\varepsilon>0$ be the small parameter guaranteed to exist by Theorem~\ref{thm on uniform density estimates on families of hypersurfaces}. There exists a constant $C\in\mathbb{R}^+$ such that for all $t\in[a,b]$, $\phi\in C^1_{\mathrm{b}}(\overline{U})$ with $\lVert\phi\rVert_{C^{0,1}(\overline{U})}\leqslant\varepsilon$, $0<\delta\leqslant\varepsilon$, and $x,z\in U$ the following items hold, where we implement the shorthand
  \begin{equation}\label{short_hand}
    \{{|\Psi + \phi - t|\leqslant\delta}\} = \{{x\in U\;:\;|\Psi(x) + \phi(x) - t|\leqslant\delta}\}.
  \end{equation}
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item We have the bounds
    \begin{equation}\label{thin_est_1}
      \frac{1}{\delta}\int_{\{{|\Psi + \phi - t|\leqslant\delta}\}}|\lambda^q(x - y)|\;\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^n(y)\leqslant C\begin{cases}
        1&\text{if }0\leqslant q<n-1,\\
        1+|\log\delta|&\text{if }q = n-1,\\
        \delta^{n-1-q}&\text{if }n-1<q<n.
      \end{cases}
    \end{equation}
    \item If $0\leqslant q<n-1$, we have the uniform continuity estimates 
    \begin{equation}\label{thin_est_2}
      \frac{1}{\delta}\int_{\{{|\Psi + \phi - t|\leqslant\delta}\}}|\lambda^q(x - y) - \lambda^q(z - y)|\;\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^n(y)\leqslant C\begin{cases}
        |x - z|&\text{if }0\leqslant q<n-2,\\
        |x - z|(1 + |\log|x - z||)&\text{if }q = n-2,\\
        |x - z|^{n-1-q}&\text{if }n-2<q<n-1.
      \end{cases}
    \end{equation}
  \end{enumerate}
<!-- end coro-6 -->
<!-- proof-37: body_md -->
To prove the first item, we begin by splitting the integral and employing the coarea formula; for a reference to the latter, the reader is referred to, e.g., Theorem 3.2.12 in Federer~\cite{MR257325}. The equality
  \begin{multline}\label{coarea_equality}
    \frac{1}{\delta}\int_{\{{|\Psi + \phi - t|\leqslant\delta}\}}|\lambda^q(x - y)|\;\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^n(y) =\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{\{{|\Psi + \phi - t|\leqslant\delta}\}\cap B(x,\delta)}|\lambda^q(x - y)|\;\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^n(y)\\+ \frac{1}{\delta}\int_{-\delta}^\delta\int_{M(\phi,t+s)\setminus B(x,\delta)}\frac{|\lambda^q(x - y)|}{|\nabla(\Psi + \phi)(y)|}\;\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(y)\;\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^1(s)
  \end{multline}
  is obtained; note that we are tacitly using the first and second items of Theorem~\ref{thm on uniform density estimates on families of hypersurfaces}.
  
  The first term on the right of~\eqref{coarea_equality} is estimated using radial spherical coordinates (e.g. 3.2.13 in~\cite{MR257325}):
  \begin{multline}\label{EE_1}
    \frac{1}{\delta}\int_{\{{|\Psi + \phi - t|\leqslant\delta}\}\cap B(x,\delta)}|\lambda^q(x - y)|\;\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^n(y)\leqslant \frac{2\pi^{n/2}}{\Upgamma(n/2)}\frac{1}{\delta}\int_0^\delta s^{n-1}|\lambda^q(s)|\;\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^1(s)\\\leqslant C\begin{cases}
    \delta^{n-1-q}&\text{if }0<q<n,\\
    \delta^{n-1}(1 + |\log\delta|)&\text{if }q = 0.
    \end{cases}
  \end{multline}
  The second term on the right hand side of~\eqref{coarea_equality} is instead estimated via the third, fourth, and fifth items of Theorem~\ref{thm on uniform density estimates on families of hypersurfaces}:
  \begin{equation}\label{EE_2}
    \frac{1}{\delta}\int_{-\delta}^\delta\int_{M(\phi,t+s)\setminus B(x,\delta)}\frac{|\lambda^q(x - y)|}{|\nabla(\Psi + \phi)(y)|}\;\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(y)\;\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^1(s)\leqslant C\begin{cases}
      1 &\text{if }0\leqslant q<n-1,\\
      1 + |\log\delta|&\text{if }q = n-1,\\
      \delta^{n-1 -q}&\text{if }n-1<q<n.
    \end{cases}
  \end{equation}
  The proof of the first item is completed by combining inequalities~\eqref{EE_1} and~\eqref{EE_2}.

  We now prove the second item. It is sufficient to address the case that $x\neq z$. Define the region
  \begin{equation}\label{the_important_disk}
    D(x,z) = \{{y\in\mathbb{R}^n\;:\;|y - (x+z)/2|\leqslant2|x - z|}\}.
  \end{equation}
  The following simple observations are useful. The inclusion
  \begin{equation}\label{observation_1}
    D(x,z)\subset B(x,5|x - z|/2)\cap B(z,5|x - z|/2)
  \end{equation}
  holds. The estimates
  \begin{equation}\label{observation_2}
    \forall\;y\in\mathbb{R}^n\setminus D(x,z),\;\forall\;\tau\in[0,1],\;|\tau x + (1 - \tau)z - y|\geqslant 3|y - (x + z)/2|/4.
  \end{equation}
  hold.


  We now split the integral on the left hand side of~\eqref{thin_est_2} according to the region~\eqref{the_important_disk}. When $y\in D(x,z)$, we do not exploit the difference and instead just use the coarea formula again, the second and fourth items of Theorem~\ref{thm on uniform density estimates on families of hypersurfaces}, and observation~\eqref{observation_1}. In doing so, we learn that
  \begin{equation}\label{part_1}
    \frac{1}{\delta}\int_{\{{|\Psi + \phi - t|\leqslant\delta}\}\cap D(x,z)}|\lambda^q(x - y) - \lambda^q(z - y)|\;\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^n(y)\leqslant C\begin{cases}
    |x - z|^{n-1}(1 + |\log|x - z||)&\text{if }q = 0,\\
    |x - z|^{n-1-q}&\text{if }0<q<n-1.
    \end{cases}
  \end{equation}
  
  On the other hand, when $y\in \mathbb{R}^n\setminus D(x,z)$, we do exploit the difference between $\lambda^q(x-y)$ and $\lambda^q(z-y)$ via the fundamental theorem of calculus. Thanks to observation~\eqref{observation_2}, this results in the bound
  \begin{equation}\label{diff_bou}
    |\lambda^q(x - y) - \lambda^q(z - y)|\leqslant C|x - z||\lambda^{q+1}(y - (x+z)/2)|.
  \end{equation}
  Upon combining~\eqref{diff_bou} with the coarea formula and the second, fourth, and fifth items of Theorem~\ref{thm on uniform density estimates on families of hypersurfaces}, one finds that 
  \begin{equation}\label{part_2}
    \frac{1}{\delta}\int_{\{{|\Psi + \phi - t|\leqslant\delta}\}\setminus D(x,z)}|\lambda^q(x - y) - \lambda^q(z - y)|\;\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n}(y)\leqslant C\begin{cases}
      |x - z|&\text{if }q<n-2,\\
      |x - z|(1 + |\log|x - z||)&\text{if }q = n-2,\\
      |x - z|^{n-1-q}&\text{if }n-2<q<n-1.
    \end{cases}
  \end{equation}
  Summing~\eqref{part_1} with~\eqref{part_2} completes the proof of the second item.
<!-- end proof-37 -->
<!-- prop-9: body_md -->
\label{prop on transverse cylinder density estimate}
  Let $\mathbb{N}\ni n\geqslant 2$, $\varnothing\neq U\subset\mathbb{R}^n$ be open and bounded, $\Psi\in C^1_{\mathrm{b}}(\overline{U})$, $a,b\in\mathbb{R}$ with $a\leqslant b$, and assume that~\eqref{hypotheses_are_here} holds. Fix $p\in\mathbb{R}^n$ and $\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$ and assume additionally that for some $\sigma_0\in\mathbb{R}$ we have $p + \sigma_0\nu\in E$ and
  \begin{equation}\label{transverse intersection condition}
    \forall\;\sigma\in\mathbb{R},\;p + \sigma\nu\in E\;\Rightarrow\;\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi(p + \sigma\nu)\neq0.
  \end{equation}
  Then, there exist constants $C,\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}^+$ such that for all $a-\varepsilon\leqslant t\leqslant b + \varepsilon$, $\phi\in C^1(\overline{U})$ satisfying $\lVert\phi\rVert_{C^{0,1}(\overline{U})}\leqslant\varepsilon$, and $\rho\in\mathbb{R}^+$ we have the estimate
  \begin{equation}\label{cylindrical_density_estimate}
    \mathcal{H}^{n-1}(M(\phi,t)\cap\{{x\in\mathbb{R}^n\;:\;|\mathbb{P}(x - p)|\leqslant\rho}\})\leqslant C\rho^{n-1}
  \end{equation}
  where $M(\phi,t)\Subset U$ is the hypersurface defined on the left hand side of equation~\eqref{INC_1} and $\mathbb{P} = I - \nu\otimes\nu$.
<!-- end prop-9 -->
<!-- proof-38: body_md -->
We begin by arguing in a similar fashion to the first part of the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm on uniform density estimates on families of hypersurfaces}. Let $\hat{a}$, $\hat{b}$, and $\hat{E}$ be as in~\eqref{fat_set}, but satisfying additionally
  \begin{equation}\label{additional_condition}
    \forall\;\sigma\in\mathbb{R}\text{ such that }p + \sigma \nu\in\hat{E},\text{ we have }\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi(p + \sigma\nu)\neq 0.
  \end{equation}
  After shrinking the enlargement in~\eqref{fat_set}, if necessary, this condition can be arranged by continuity and compactness. Indeed, otherwise there would exist a sequence $x_j=p+\sigma_j\nu\in\overline U$ such that $\mathrm{dist}(\Psi(x_j),[a,b])\to0$ and $\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi(x_j)=0$. A convergent subsequence would have a limit in $E$, contradicting~\eqref{transverse intersection condition}.

  Set $\mathcal{K}=\Psi(\hat E)$. For each $s\in\mathcal{K}$ we again define the map $\Psi_s\in C^1_{\mathrm{b}}(\overline{U})$ via $\Psi_s(x) = \Psi(x) - s$ and the nonempty set $M_s=\{{x\in U\;:\;\Psi_s(x) = 0}\}$. Again, each $\Psi_s$ is within the scope of Theorem~\ref{thm on stable local level set coordinates}; invoking this result gives us positive constants $C_s,r_s,\varepsilon_s,\widetilde{\varepsilon}_s,\hat{\varepsilon}_s\in\mathbb{R}^+$ (with $C_s\geqslant 1$) and a function $\Theta^s_\phi$ such that the inequalities of equation~\eqref{AI_SLOP} and the two items listed in that part of the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm on uniform density estimates on families of hypersurfaces} are true. By also appealing to the third item of Lemma~\ref{lem on regularized distance to the manifold} in the process, we can enlarge the constant $C_s$, if necessary, to arrange that 
  \begin{equation}\label{we_need_this}
    |\Theta_\phi^s(m,\tau) - m|\leqslant C_s|\Psi_s(m) + \phi(m) - \tau|\text{ for all }(m,\tau)\in M_s\times[-\hat{\varepsilon}_s,\hat{\varepsilon}_s].
  \end{equation}

  Before we appeal to compactness in some way to reduce to a finite number of model level sets, we first need to import the conclusions of Lemma~\ref{lem on reverse Lipschitz bound with projection}. 
  
  We now note that for any $s\in \mathcal{K}$, the set 
  \begin{equation}
    L_s = \{{x\in M_s\;:\;\exists\;\sigma\in\mathbb{R}\;:\;p + \sigma\nu = x}\}
  \end{equation}
  is necessarily finite: $L_s$ is evidently compact and each member of this set is isolated. Indeed, for each $x\in L_s$ the condition~\eqref{additional_condition} guarantees that $\nu\cdot\nabla\Psi_s(x)\neq0$. The implicit function theorem can then be applied to completely describe the zero set $M_s$ in an open neighborhood of $x$ as a graph $y\mapsto y + \nu\varphi(y)$ for $y\cdot\nu = 0$ and $\varphi$ a continuously differentiable scalar function. Such a local graph can only have one intersection with the line $\{{p + \sigma\nu\;:\;\sigma\in\mathbb{R}}\}$.

  With $s\in\mathcal{K}$ fixed, if $L_s\neq\varnothing$, we can apply Lemma~\ref{lem on reverse Lipschitz bound with projection} (whose hypotheses are satisfied thanks again to~\eqref{additional_condition}) a finite number of times, once for each member of $L_s$. After executing this procedure, we acquire positive parameters $\kappa_s,\delta_s,\mathsf{C}_s\in\mathbb{R}^+$, with $\delta_s<\min\{{\varepsilon_s,r_s}\}$, such that for all $\hat{m}\in L_s$, $m,\widetilde{m}\in M_s$, $\phi\in C^1_{\mathrm{b}}(\overline{U})$, and $\tau\in\mathbb{R}$ obeying the inequalities 
  \begin{equation}
    \max\{{|m - \hat{m}|,|\widetilde{m} - \hat{m}|}\}\leqslant\kappa_s,\;\max\{{\lVert\phi\rVert_{C^{0,1}(\overline{U})},|\tau|}\}\leqslant\delta_s
  \end{equation} 
  we have the reverse Lipschitz estimate
  \begin{equation}\label{REV_rev_LIP}
    |m - \widetilde{m}|\leqslant\mathsf{C}_s|\mathbb{P}(\Theta^s_\phi(m,\tau) - \Theta^s_\phi(\widetilde{m},\tau))|,
  \end{equation}
  where $\mathbb{P} = I - \nu\otimes\nu$. If $L_s=\varnothing$, choose any $\kappa_s,\mathsf C_s\in\mathbb{R}^+$ and $0<\delta_s<\min\{{\varepsilon_s,r_s}\}$; the preceding assertion is then vacuous.

  We shall also require the following simple auxiliary fact. For each $s\in\mathcal K$, there exists $0<\hat{\kappa}_s\leqslant\kappa_s/2$ with the property that 
  \begin{equation}\label{simple_auxiliary_fact}
    M_s\cap\{{x\in\mathbb{R}^n\;:\;|\mathbb{P}(x - p)|\leqslant2\hat{\kappa}_s}\}\subset\bigcup_{\hat{m}\in L_s}B(\hat{m},\kappa_s).
  \end{equation}
  If $L_s=\varnothing$, this follows from compactness, since $\min_{m\in M_s}|\mathbb{P}(m-p)|>0$. If $L_s\neq\varnothing$, this assertion is readily proved via contradiction. If no such $\hat{\kappa}_s$ existed, then we could construct a sequence $\{{x_\mu}\}_{\mu = 0}^\infty\subset M_s$ satisfying
  \begin{equation}\label{the condition on the}
    \lim_{\mu\to\infty}|\mathbb{P}(x_\mu - p)| = 0\text{ and }\forall\;\hat{m}\in L_s,\;\inf_{\mu\in\mathbb{N}}|x_\mu - \hat{m}|\geqslant\kappa_s.
  \end{equation}
  Thanks to the compactness of $M_s$, we can extract a convergent subsequence to some limit point $x_\infty\in M_s$. The condition on the left hand side of~\eqref{the condition on the} ensures that for some $\sigma\in\mathbb{R}$ we must have $x_\infty = p + \sigma\nu$. But then $x_\infty\in L_s$, which is impossible by the rightmost condition of~\eqref{the condition on the}. So the sought-after $0<\hat{\kappa}_s\leqslant\kappa_s/2$ indeed exists.

  The corresponding open intervals cover the compact set $\mathcal K$. Hence there exist a finite set $\varnothing\neq\digamma\subset\mathcal K$ and a number $\varepsilon_0>0$ such that
  \begin{equation}\label{yet_another_compactness}
    \{{t\in\mathbb{R}\;:\;\mathrm{dist}(t,\mathcal K)\leqslant\varepsilon_0}\}\subset\bigcup_{s\in\digamma}\Big\{{t\in\mathbb{R}\;:\;|t - s|<\min\Big\{{\delta_s,\frac{\hat{\kappa}_s}{2C_s}}\Big\}}\Big\}.
  \end{equation}
  Let $\varepsilon_{\mathrm d}>0$ be an admissible perturbation size in Theorem~\ref{thm on uniform density estimates on families of hypersurfaces}, applied with $q=0$. By using this finite set $\digamma$, we may then instantiate the finite and positive parameters
  \begin{multline}
    \varepsilon=\min\Big\{{\varepsilon_0,\varepsilon_{\mathrm d},\frac{a-\hat a}{3},\frac{\hat b-b}{3},
      \min\Big\{{\min\Big\{{\delta_s,\frac{\hat{\kappa}_s}{2C_s}}\Big\}\;:\;s\in\digamma}\Big\}}\Big\},\;
    \varrho=\min\{{\hat{\kappa}_s\;:\;s\in\digamma}\},\\\text{and }
    \mathsf{C}=\max\{{\max\{{\mathsf{C}_s,C_s}\}\;:\;s\in\digamma}\}.
  \end{multline}

  We are now ready to prove the density estimate~\eqref{cylindrical_density_estimate}. Let $t\in[a - \varepsilon,b +\varepsilon]$, $\phi\in C^1_{\mathrm{b}}(\overline{U})$ satisfy $\lVert\phi\rVert_{C^{0,1}(\overline{U})}\leqslant\varepsilon$, and $\rho\in\mathbb{R}^+$. Consider first the case that $\rho\geqslant\varrho$. Then, we crudely bound the Hausdorff measure on the left hand side of~\eqref{cylindrical_density_estimate} as
  \begin{equation}
    \mathcal{H}^{n-1}(M(\phi,t)\cap\{{x\in\mathbb{R}^n\;:\;|\mathbb{P}(x - p)|\leqslant\rho}\})\leqslant\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(M(\phi,t))\leqslant(\overline{C}/\varrho^{n-1})\rho^{n-1},
  \end{equation}
  where $\overline{C}\in\mathbb{R}^+$ is the finite positive constant obtained from the right hand side of the third item of Theorem~\ref{thm on uniform density estimates on families of hypersurfaces} (evaluated when the radius is taken to be $\mathrm{diam}(U)$). 

  Now suppose that $0<\rho<\varrho$. If the set on the left-hand side of~\eqref{cylindrical_density_estimate} is empty, there is nothing to prove. Otherwise, choose $x_\ast$ in this set. Then $x_\ast\in\hat E$ and $\mathrm{dist}(t,\mathcal K)\leqslant|\phi(x_\ast)|\leqslant\varepsilon_0$. From the finite covering~\eqref{yet_another_compactness}, there exists $s\in\digamma$ with
    $|t - s|<\min\{{\delta_s,\hat\kappa_s/(2C_s)}\}<\varepsilon_s$. In turn, we may invoke the inclusion~\eqref{some_inclusions}, the identity~\eqref{lev_par}, and the estimate~\eqref{bi_lip} to deduce that
  \begin{equation}
    M(\phi,t)\cap\{{x\in\mathbb{R}^n\;:\;|\mathbb{P}(x - p)|\leqslant\rho}\} = \{{\Theta^s_\phi(m,t - s)\;:\;m\in \widetilde{M}_s(\rho)}\}
  \end{equation} 
  where
  \begin{equation}
    \widetilde{M}_s(\rho) = \{{m\in M_s\;:\;|\mathbb{P}(\Theta_\phi^s(m,t - s) - p)|\leqslant\rho}\}.
  \end{equation}
  By Theorem 2.8 in Evans and Gariepy~\cite{MR3409135} and the Lipschitz bounds~\eqref{bi_lip}, we thus have
  \begin{equation}
    \mathcal{H}^{n-1}(M(\phi,t)\cap\{{x\in\mathbb{R}^n\;:\;|\mathbb{P}(x - p)|\leqslant\rho}\})\leqslant\mathsf{C}^{n-1}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\widetilde{M}_s(\rho)).
  \end{equation}
  So it suffices to measure $\widetilde{M}_s(\rho)$. Notice that if $m\in\widetilde{M}_s(\rho)$, then from estimate~\eqref{we_need_this}, we get 
  \begin{equation}
    |\mathbb{P}(m - p)|\leqslant\rho + |\Theta_\phi^s(m,t-s) - m|\leqslant\rho+C_s(\lVert\phi\rVert_{C^{0,1}(\overline U)}+|t-s|)<2\hat{\kappa}_s.
  \end{equation}
  In turn, appealing to the inclusion~\eqref{simple_auxiliary_fact}, we learn that 
  \begin{equation}
    \widetilde{M}_s(\rho) \subset\bigcup_{\hat{m}\in L_s}B(\hat{m},\kappa_s)\cap\widetilde{M}_s(\rho).
  \end{equation}
  For every $\hat m\in L_s$ for which $B(\hat m,\kappa_s)\cap\widetilde{M}_s(\rho)$ is nonempty, choose
  $m_{\hat m}\in B(\hat m,\kappa_s)\cap\widetilde{M}_s(\rho)$. If $m$ belongs to the set $B(\hat{m},\kappa_s)\cap\widetilde{M}_s(\rho)$ as well, then~\eqref{REV_rev_LIP} gives
  \begin{equation}
    |m-m_{\hat m}|\leqslant\mathsf C_s|\mathbb{P}(\Theta_\phi^s(m,t-s)-\Theta_\phi^s(m_{\hat m},t-s))|\leqslant2\mathsf C_s\rho.
  \end{equation}
  Consequently,
  \begin{equation}
    B(\hat m,\kappa_s)\cap\widetilde{M}_s(\rho)\subset B(m_{\hat m},2\mathsf C_s\rho)\cap M_s.
  \end{equation}
  Synthesizing these observations, we deduce that
  \begin{equation}
    \mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\widetilde{M}_s(\rho))\leqslant\sum_{\substack{\hat{m}\in L_s\\B(\hat m,\kappa_s)\cap\widetilde{M}_s(\rho)\neq\varnothing}}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(B(m_{\hat m},2\mathsf{C}_s\rho)\cap M_s).
  \end{equation}
  The desired density estimate~\eqref{cylindrical_density_estimate} now follows from Lemma~\ref{lem on density estimates on a fixed manifold}, since $\digamma$ is finite and each $L_s$ is finite.
<!-- end proof-38 -->
